Unit tests `tests/coord.test.ts` (14), including a verbatim copy of Ather's `parseEditorLock` reading holder/until/session from the D2 lines. - S2 (rev 2, 2026-10-06): A1. With A5 off: the Editor gate (lock, PIE RAM, save-all) refuses nothing; the pane hook returns Ather's tree untouched; the status line is cleared once and then left to Ather; the minute timer probes nothing; no toast (the 🟥 toast included); the Editor section of the model's rules moved from `rules-flow.md` to `rules-a5.md`. 🟥/⏯️ title marks, unread and PENDING.md stay. - S3 (rev 2, 2026-10-06): Editor holder wired in `register.ts`. The minute tick (and every `editor` tool call, serialized with it) reads the lock, every `Saved/HaiFlow/editor/*.json`, Ather's lanes, `sync.json`, the touch files and one machine probe, writes only this session's file (heartbeat, request, lease, yield asks, delivered notice ids), and decides with `coord.ts`. Grant/recovery/extend/release write the lock by read-compare-write and a re-read. Model tool `mcp__hai-flow__editor` (request/release/extend/status), registered only once A5 is seen on. Holder side: lease-end (end − 5), overrun (end), idle 15 min, yield asks addressed to it. Waiter side: stale-lease recovery (holder gone, no Editor), yield ask once per holder per hour (in its own file for a hai-flow holder; the standard `UE request:` line through `$.session.send` to a holder without hai-flow), a gone holder with a live Editor reported. Under A5, Edit/Write/redirects into `Saved/EDITOR_OWNER.txt` are refused naming the tool; the lock gate's hint names the tool. Notices (D6): queued once per id; mid-turn they ride the main loop's next tool result as `context`; idle and actionable → one `$.prompt.submit`; waiting ones ride Hai's next prompt as context; an Edit/Write adding a `hai-flow ·` line to `docs/intent/**` is refused. Bug found and fixed by the tests: the mod's own `$.tool.call` (session title) passed through its own hook and marked the session busy (and could have drained notices into a call no model reads): "main loop" is now `agentId === undefined && next.origin.plugin === 'engine'` (measured in the test harness: the mod's calls carry origin `hai-flow`, with an engine-minted tool_use_id). Shared test world moved to `tests/world.ts`; engine tests in `tests/coord-engine.test.ts`. Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `66 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A3 met.
- S4 (rev 2, 2026-10-06): RAM, in the same tick: before a grant (and at most every 5 min while a slot waits on RAM) the head runs the safe cleanup: `Stop-Process` of LiveCodingConsole only when a fresh probe shows no UnrealEditor, the S2 reaper `reap-orphan-git.ps1` under 14 GB; other heavy processes are named in the wait/grant text and never killed. Launch gate = panel override in the plugin store over the new options `launchGatePieGb` (31) / `launchGateGb` (28); an open Editor is reused without it. PIE start 5 GB unchanged (existing gate); abort notice under 3 GB while this session's PIE runs (once per episode); the checkout drive under 20 GB reported once a day. Engine tests with mocked probes: cleanup + wait naming memory holders; LiveCodingConsole kept with an Editor and the open Editor reused; gate from options; panel gate wins; abort + disk notices once. Evidence: same run, `66 pass 0 fail`. Acceptance: A4 met.

- S5 (rev 2, 2026-10-06): Sync main holder. `/a5 sync HH:MM [for <id8|lane>]`, `/a5 sync move HH:MM | cancel | done [note] | abort <why> | takeover`, `/a5 sync` (status); model tool `mcp__hai-flow__sync` (plan, move, cancel, conflicts, done, abort, status), one `syncAction` behind both (and the panel in S7). `Saved/HaiFlow/sync.json` is written by its holder alone (the planner writes it once when it names another holder; a gone holder's sync can be taken over); a write is skipped if another session became holder since the read. Each session's tick: named-holder notice, cutoff checkpoint notice at T − 30 (holder and non-holder wording), release-by notice to an Editor holder from T − 10, freeze notice from T (holder: run s2-sync-main, then done/abort), lift notice on done/aborted/cancelled to the sessions that were told of the sync (superseding their pending cutoff/freeze notices). The holder's hai-flow dry-runs `git -C <S2> merge-tree --write-tree --name-only HEAD origin/main` at the cutoff (60 s), classifies each path by rule 11 (main's blob vs every blob in `git log --raw HEAD -- <path>`; first 40 paths, the rest foreign), writes the conflicts; owners are the sessions whose `touch/<id8>.json` lists the path (each hai-flow records the shared-checkout paths its session and workers edit); owners get the self/foreign wording, the holder the list with owners and the failure-table rule. Freeze: from T until done/abort, a non-holder's git writes (commit, add, rm, mv, checkout, switch, restore, reset, stash, merge, rebase, pull, push, cherry-pick, revert, clean, am) in the shared S2 checkout and every Editor use (Unreal MCP, Editor start/stop) are refused; reads, other repos and own worktrees pass; `sync.json` is read fresh for each such call. Grants crossing the cutoff are deferred (E1). Status line shows `Sync HH:MM cutoff|frozen`. Engine tests `tests/sync-engine.test.ts` (10): plan/move/cancel and holder-only changes; named holder; cutoff notice once; deferral; dry-run + rule 11 + owner routing; foreign wording to the owner; freeze and lift; holder not frozen and ends it by tool; takeover of a gone holder. Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `76 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A5 met. Findings F-2, F-3 (non-blocking).

- S6 (rev 2, 2026-10-06): notices reviewed against D6. Gaps closed: a shell command that writes a `hai-flow ·` line into `docs/intent/**` (redirect, tee, Out-File/Set-Content/Add-Content, heredoc bodies included) is refused like an Edit/Write/MultiEdit; the "rides Hai's next prompt as context" path is tested. Tests added: "notices: one waiting for the next turn rides Hai's next prompt as context, once"; "notices: Ather's intent log and findings never take a hai-flow line, by edit or by shell" (log.md by MultiEdit, findings.md by `echo >>`, log.md by `Add-Content`; a plain progress line passes). With S3/S5: one idle prompt per event (queue, lease, yield, cutoff, lift tests), mid-turn context on the main loop's result and never a worker's, no cross-session sends between hai-flow sessions. - S9 (rev 3, 2026-10-06): A9 shared machine probe. `readWorld` no longer runs PowerShell itself: `sharedProbe` reads `Saved/HaiFlow/probe.json` (`{ at, by, probe }`) and uses it while it is younger than 50 s; when older, the session claims it by read-compare-write (re-read equal to the first read, write a claim carrying the old reading, re-read equal to the claim), probes, and writes the reading; a lost claim uses the other session's file. `freshProbe` (grant, cleanup, release) always runs the probe and publishes it. A grant now re-decides on a fresh reading before taking the lock. Tests: "A9: while another session keeps the shared reading fresh, this one never probes; once it is stale, it probes once per period and shares it" (0 probes over 3 min while another session refreshes, then 1 per tick, file written `by` this session); "A9: a grant reads the machine itself, even when the shared reading is fresh" (shared 40 GB, machine 20 GB → waits under the 28 GB gate, the fresh reading is shared). README RAM row and file list updated. Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `84 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A9 met.

- S10 (rev 3, 2026-10-06): A10 lease follows /clear. A `session.end` hook (reason `clear`, S2 sessions) records the old id and polls `$.session.id()` every 200 ms (25 tries, as Ather's `followClear`), then runs a tick; `restoreMe` sees the id change and `moveAfterClear` moves, inside the serialized tick: this session's file (request with its `requestedAt`, lease, yield asks, delivered ids) to `editor/<new id8>.json`, leaving the old file with no request, no lease and heartbeat 0; the touch paths; the lock line by read-compare-write, replacing `session <old id8>` with the new one (every other field kept, so Ather reads the new id); an open sync it holds (holder and `plannedBy`). Ather's own lane move is untouched. Residual risk (README "Giới hạn"): in the ≤ 5 s before the move, Ather's ended lane makes the old id look gone; a waiting head whose tick lands then, with no Editor running, could free the lease first (the move's compare then fails and is logged). Tests (`tests/sync-engine.test.ts`): "A10: after /clear the lease, its lock line and the sync it holds move to the new session id" (lock line identical but `session cd34ef56`; new file holds the lease; old file emptied; sync holder and plannedBy moved; MCP save allowed; no named-holder/gone/stale notice; release works); "A10: a pending request keeps its place in the queue after /clear". Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `86 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A10 met.

- S11 (rev 3, 2026-10-06): A11 untracked files main would overwrite. At the cutoff the holder's dry-run also runs `git -C <S2> diff --name-only -z --diff-filter=A HEAD origin/main` (60 s; `-z` so no path quoting), checks each added path with `$.fs.exists` in batches of 50 (at most 5,000; never a whole-tree untracked scan), and writes the hits into `sync.json` (`untracked`, reset when the sync moves). Each session whose touch file names one gets the owner notice (commit it, then it is an ordinary conflict, or move it out of the tree; never delete); the holder gets the whole list with owners or "owner unknown → 🟥 to Hai". A failed listing tells the holder to run it by hand. Sync tile and `/a5 sync` status show the count/list. Tests (`tests/sync-engine.test.ts`): "A11: at the cutoff the holder lists the files main adds that already exist on disk, each to the session that wrote it" (mocked diff with 4 added paths, 3 on disk; one `--diff-filter=A` run, no `ls-files`/`status`/`--others`; owner and holder wording); "A11: a session whose touch file names an untracked file main adds is told by its own hai-flow". README and `rules-a5.md` updated. Final gates for rev 3: `claude plugin validate` → `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `claude plugin test` → `88 pass 0 fail` (6 files); `git -C D:/Projects/ather-mods diff --stat main -- ather-automata` → empty. Evidence: commit below. Acceptance: A11 met; A1–A8 still green on the same run.

- S12 (rev 4, 2026-10-06): A13 freeze lease (D8). Every sync carries `build` and `hardEnd` (T + `syncMergeMinutes` 45, T + `syncBuildMinutes` 90 with a build; new options; a 0.4 sync.json gets the default end). `syncPhase` returns `expired` past the hard end, or while frozen once the holder session is gone (files only; before T a gone holder can still be taken over), so every reader lifts the freeze at once without waiting for a writer. The first tick that sees it writes `state: expired` with the reason (a terminal state any session may write, read-compare-write; writing it twice is harmless); the session whose `Saved/HaiFlow/alerts/<key>.json` claim wins raises the one 🟥 (title, unread, PENDING.md line via the existing marker code); every session told of the sync gets the expired notice (it supersedes pending cutoff/freeze notices). The holder is told 10 min before the hard end. `/a5 sync HH:MM [build]`, `/a5 sync build on|off`, tool `sync` action `build` / field `build`, panel Button `Build: yes/no` (holder, before the freeze); status, tile and plan texts name the hard end. Two 0.4 tests changed by D8: the timeline unit test (frozen until done/abort → expired at the hard end / holder gone) and the takeover test (a frozen sync with a gone holder now expires; takeover is tested before T). New `tests/lease-engine.test.ts`: hard end → expired + notice + one 🟥 (alert file, one PENDING line) + git open; holder gone → expired at once + 🟥; hard end from options and with a build, holder told 10 min before. Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `91 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A13 met.

- S13 (rev 4, 2026-10-06): A14 merge guard (D8). In `freezeProblem`, before the freeze: a git write whose repository root is the shared S2 checkout, while `<S2>/.git/MERGE_HEAD` exists, is refused to every session but the holder of the last sync (any state, so it can still finish or abort its own merge), naming the holder, the sync and its phase, or "no sync is open (a merge left behind)" — independent of the sync's phase; reads, other repositories and own worktrees pass; the guard lifts the moment MERGE_HEAD is gone. `mergeGuardStep` in the tick: MERGE_HEAD with no open sync → one 🟥 for Hai (alert file keyed by MERGE_HEAD's mtime; title, unread, one PENDING.md line). Freeze refusals now name the hard end. Test world: `fs.stat` answers for any in-memory file. Tests in `tests/lease-engine.test.ts`: refused with an expired sync on file (commit and `merge --abort`), reads/worktree pass, lifted when MERGE_HEAD goes; left-behind merge → one 🟥, commit refused, the last holder may `merge --abort`. Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `93 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A14 met.

- S14 (rev 4, 2026-10-06): A15 the sync's own commands pass A5. `coord.isSyncCommandOnly(command)`: every segment must be git (env assignments such as `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 GCM_INTERACTIVE=never` allowed; no nested shell, no other program), each either a read or exactly one of `merge [--no-edit|--no-ff|--no-commit] origin/main`, `merge --abort`, `checkout --ours|--theirs -- <paths>`, `revert -m 1 <sha> [--no-edit]`, `add -- <paths>`, `commit [-m <msg>|--no-edit|-F <file>|-q]` (no `.`/`*`/`-A` paths, no `--amend`, no `-a`). In the `tool.call` hook, an A5 ask (never a deny) is dropped only when the command is sync work and this session holds the sync in its frozen phase (sync.json read fresh), so the holder's worker is no longer refused on `checkout --ours`; `reset --hard`, `stash`, `clean` and every other git keep their rules; non-holders stay frozen. Tests: unit (10 allowed forms, 15 refused ones) in `tests/coord.test.ts`; engine in `tests/lease-engine.test.ts`: the holder's worker runs checkout --ours / add -- / commit / merge --abort / revert -m 1 in the freeze, is still stopped on reset --hard and stash, the holder's main loop still asks on reset --hard; before T the worker is refused (no ask for workers), a non-holder is frozen. Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `96 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A15 met.

- S15 (rev 4, 2026-10-06): A12 sync worker (D7). `$.agent.register` of `hai-flow:sync` (background; system prompt `SYNC_WORKER_PROMPT`; the session's tools and CLAUDE.md, so S2 AGENTS.md and the machine profile apply), hidden from the model by an `agent.offer` hook. At T the holder's tick writes `workerAt` to sync.json first (so a reload or a second tick never spawns again), then `$.agent.spawn`s the worker with `syncWorkerTask` (checkout, T, hard end, build, dry-run conflicts and untracked files, the lock line); the worker id comes from the spawn result, else from `$.agent.list()` (newest `hai-flow:sync` spawned by hai-flow). A refused spawn → the holder is told to run it by hand. The holder's freeze notice now says the worker runs it (no prompt into the holder's conversation). When a worker's turn ends (`turn.complete` with its agent id, or an agent whose type is the sync worker when no id was recorded) and the sync is still open, it is aborted for it with the worker's last words and the holder told. Procedure (prompt): git with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` / `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never` and tool timeouts; exactly the A15 git writes; the Editor lock first (`launch: false`, a new request field that skips the RAM launch gate for a slot that never launches); another lane's Editor never closed/killed/driven, wait and abort 10 min before the hard end; an open Editor closed only with the lock free/ours after a dirty check (dirty → abort); pre-merge HEAD recorded; merge (never rebase), rule 11 self-conflicts to ours, additive text resolved, logic or .uasset/.umap → merge --abort and abort; failed build → `git revert -m 1`; release the lock; end with sync done/abort. The holder's cutoff and named-holder notices now point at the worker. Tests (`tests/lease-engine.test.ts`): registration (procedure words), not offered to the model, spawned once at T with the sync's facts, worker end without done → aborted + holder told, done → done and no abort; the 0.4 holder test now checks the worker instead of "run s2-sync-main yourself". Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `99 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A12 met.

- S16 (rev 4, 2026-10-06): A16 session overview. The tick reads the client's own list (`$.tool.call` of `mcp__ccd_session_mgmt__list_sessions`, limit 50, at most every 5 min); a missing or refused tool, or an answer that is not a session list, leaves it null and the overview falls back to Ather's lanes and hai-flow's files. Measured on this machine (read-only, 2 rows): rows carry `sessionId` (`local_<uuid>`, the client's id, not the CLI session id the files use), `title`, `cwd`, `isArchived`, `isRunning`, `lastActivityAt`; so counts come from the client list and identities from lanes/files. Ather lanes now also give `intent` and `branch`. `overviewOf`: active client sessions (running or active within 30 min, plus this one), S2 sessions (live lanes ∪ fresh hai-flow files ∪ this one), elsewhere (active rows whose folder is neither the checkout nor an S2 worktree), counts by intent, the Editor holder and the queue, the sync holder and phase, and `withoutModOf` (a live Ather lane with no fresh hai-flow file). Panel: one `hai-overview` line under the tiles (A5 on; amber when a session runs without hai-flow 0.4). At the cutoff (and during the freeze, for one that appears later) the holder `$.session.send`s each such session one standard message (by its lane's session id), records it in sync.json `messaged`, and its notice names them with delivery. The app's `app.asar` (Claude 2.19675.1.0) binds a PR it sees in the transcript (a Bash `gh pr create` result with a `/pull/N` URL, or a `<pr-created>` tag) and its GitHub client only watches existing PRs (CI, auto-merge, merge queue); no PR-creating call found. Feasibility first, MEASURED in the engine types (`a5/.claude-plugin/types/claude-code/index.d.ts`, BoxProps): `position: "absolute"` "leaves the flow, as in CSS: placed against its parent by the offsets, no room among its siblings, painted over those before it", with `top`/`left`/`right`/`bottom` ("with `right` and no `width` the Box spans the two") and `overflow: "hidden"`; and an `Svg` with `isInteractive` runs SMIL. So the curtain can lie over the text, no finding (F-4) is needed. Built: `icons.curtainSvg` (an 8×8 pattern of sixteen 2-pixel cells, each animating its opacity once at its 4×4 Bayer threshold, tiled over a 2400×480 canvas; `fill="freeze"`), laid over a block by `withCurtain` (the block becomes `position: relative`; its last child an absolute box at 0/0/0/0 with `overflow: hidden` holding the SVG). When the A5 pane opens (`/a5`, `★ A5 ›`, or its first draw) every block (band, tools, sessions, card, rules, in that order) gets a seal-red curtain that clears in Bayer order, 4 ms a cell, blocks staggered so the last cell ends at ≤ 300 ms; the compact line gets one when it first draws. The curtains stay in the tree for 1 s after the opening, then a redraw drops them (still). Desktop only (the terminal has no `Svg`), never with `motion: off`. Whether it reads as intended on the real pane is Hai's review. Tests changed by design: the tests that read a desktop block's own children (A26/A31 rows, A35 list keys, A36 rules keys) read them through `still()` (`tests/world.ts`), which drops curtain boxes; the width measure gives an absolute box no width. - S56 (rev 14, 2026-10-07): A45 + A46, one quote-aware reading of PR commands (`hooks/prcmd.ts`). The adversary review's finding is right: S50's `isPrCommand` wanted `gh` at a bare segment start, so on 0.11.2 these opened a PR unscored: `GH_TOKEN=x gh pr create`, `git push; if ($?) { gh pr create }`, `& gh pr create`, `bash -c "gh pr create"`, `env X=1 gh …`, `time gh …`, `{ gh pr create; }`, `cmd /c "…"` and a shell-fed here-document; and `prCommandRefs` split the command quote-blind, so `--body "fix a; b" --head intent/x` lost its head and scored the session's checkout. Now: `splitCommands` splits a command line outside quotes on `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, newlines and the grouping marks `{ } ( )` (`${name}` kept), a `#` that starts a word ends the line; `words` splits a command quote-aware (quotes removed, content whole); `verbOf` skips env assignments (`A=1`), `env` and its options, and lead words (`time`, `&`, `.`, `!`, `then`, `do`, `if`, `nohup`, `sudo`, `exec`, `xargs`, `call`, `start`, `iex` …), drops a path and `.exe`; a shell (`bash`, `sh`, `zsh`, `dash`, `ksh`, `cmd`, `powershell`, `pwsh`) given `-c`/`-lc`/`-Command`/`/c` is read again as a command line; `heredocs` reads here-documents exactly (the terminator is the word alone on its line, leading tabs stripped only for `<<-`; a `<<` inside quotes or after a `#` comment opens none; `<<<` is a here-string) and returns the bodies a shell reads as commands (`bash <<EOF`, `pwsh <<EOF`), every other body being data; `prCalls` finds each `gh pr create` and each `gh api …repos/<o>/<r>/pulls` that POSTs (`-X POST`, `--method=POST`, or fields/`--input` without a GET), with the folder a `cd`/`pushd`/`Set-Location` before it moved to; `prRefs` reads the call's own words: `-R o/r`, `-Ro/r`, `-R=o/r`, `--repo o/r`, `--repo=o/r` and the same for `-H/--head`, `-B/--base`; values of gh pr create's other valued flags (`--title`, `--body`, `-F`, …) are never read as flags; for `gh api` only the path and the fields (`-f/-F/--field/--raw-field`, `--field=head=x`, `-fhead=x`; its `-H` is a header) count, and a `@file` value is a problem, so the score is "not scored". `accept.isPrCommand` = any PR call; `accept.prCommandRefs(command)` = the first call's refs (the problem passes to `prTarget`, which returns "not scored"); `accept.commandText` = `heredocs().text`. Tests changed by design: `pr-branch.test.ts` calls `prCommandRefs(command)` (no tokenizer argument). New `tests/prcmd.test.ts`: 27 command forms that are a PR (each form the review listed, plus `gh.exe`, a quoted full path, `/usr/bin/gh`, `sh -lc`, `pwsh -c` with a chain, a subshell, `$( … )`, `if …; then …; fi`, both shell-fed here-documents, three POSTing api forms); 13 that are not (the F-4 forms: `echo "gh pr create"`, a `cat <<EOF` body quoting it, a commit message, comments, GET and plain api reads, `gh pr view/list`, a grep for the text); the refs in every flag form (the review's `--body "fix a; b" --head` case, `-Hfeat -Ro/r`, `-H=`, `--head=me:feat`, a body starting with `-H`, `cd "D:/x y" &&`, `Set-Location …; if ($?) { … }`, api `--field=head=`, `-fhead=`, its `-H` header ignored, `-F head=@file` → the problem, a cd inside `bash -c`); exact here-document ends (`EOFX` and ` EOF` do not end it, `<<-` strips tabs only, spaces do not count, `<<` after `#` or inside quotes opens none, `<<<`); through the gate, four of the bypass forms are refused with the acceptance list and none runs. Evidence: `✔ Validation passed`; tsc exit 0; `205 pass 0 fail`; commit below. Acceptance: A45 and A46 met.

- S61 (rev 16, 2026-10-07): A49, session rows not cut at the right edge. Cause (read from `register.paneOf` and the blocks it places): the A5R pane puts every block after the band on the gutter by adding `marginX: 2` to its props, but each of those blocks (`hai-tiles`, `hai-overview`, `hai-accept`, `hai-a5r-rules`) also carries its own `width: '100%'`. In a flex column 100% is the parent's whole content width and margins sit outside it, so each block started 2 columns in and ended 2 columns past the pane's right edge; the pane clips there, which cut the end of each session row's status (its last column, "· 2m" → "· 2") and the same 2 columns of the tools box's right rim. The row itself was right (dot, title shrinking with `minWidth: 0` and truncate-end, status `flexShrink: 0`). Fix: `paneOf` drops a gutter block's own `width` and the pane column stretches it (`alignItems: 'stretch'`), so its width is the pane's less both margins; the band keeps its full width (its gutter is padding, inside its width). Test helper `world.placed(tree, columns)`: where each keyed box lies across the pane down the column blocks (a child's `width: '100%'` or number is its width with margins outside; a child without one stretches less its margins; content less border and padding). New `tests/layout.test.ts` A49 on terminal 44/60/100 and desktop 40/60/100 columns with a long title and an `fx-sand · 2m` status: every block inside the pane (gutter blocks at x 2 to columns − 2), each session row's right edge inside its block and `minWidth(row) <= row width`, the status text whole with `flexShrink: 0`, the title the one that shrinks. Red first: with the old hooks the six A49 tests fail (`212 pass 12 fail` with A50's six); green: `claude plugin test D:/Projects/ather-mods-wt/a5r-rev16/a5r` → `224 pass 0 fail`. Cause (`register.drawA5Pane` and `watch.tilesRow`): the Memory sub-line was one joined string (`gate … · PIE needs 5 GB · <disk> · <n> git processes`) with `subWarn: extra.length > 0`, and `tilesRow` draws a sub-line as one Text in one colour, so any disk or git warning turned the whole line amber, the gate details with it. Fix: a tool's `sub` may now be a list of parts (`watch.SubPart` `{ text, warn? }`); `tilesRow` draws such a list as one quiet, wrapping Text whose warning parts are nested amber Texts, joined by " · " (the text reads exactly as before). Memory passes the gate details as plain parts and the low disk and `N git processes` as warning parts; "below launch gate" stays the value's amber note. Sync main keeps its string sub-line (not in A50). Below the gate the gate details also stay quiet (only the note and the warning parts are amber). Tests `tests/v2.test.ts` A50 on terminal and desktop: above the gate without a warning, above the gate with 10 git processes, below the gate with 10 git processes and a 12 GB disk; the amber Texts are exactly the warning parts and the line's own Text is not amber. Red first: the six fail on the old hooks; green: `claude plugin test D:/Projects/ather-mods-wt/a5r-rev16/a5r` → `224 pass 0 fail`. Acceptance: A50 met.

A52 cause (`register.postHoc`): it called `gatherAccept(root, slug, '', undefined)` with no refs, so `prTarget` scored the session's checkout at HEAD (the shared S2 checkout's `HaiHuynh/20261005`, 661 ahead), once for all new numbers, and raised a 🟥 per number on that one score; `refreshAccept` then re-scored the checkout under the same "PR #… · scored after the fact" label whenever a file changed. One score per PR: `lastAccept.prs` (number, `head → base · state · N files, read from …`, scores) and `scores` = `worstOf` them; the card draws one section per PR (`hai-accept-pr-<n>`, its own verdict); `refreshAccept` never re-scores the checkout under a PR card. A 🟥 only for a PR whose own score fails (or whose diff could not be read whole, as before), claimed as `alerts/pr2-<n>.json` with `scorer: 2`; a merged PR's question asks for a follow-up PR. `gh pr view` failing (no gh, offline): "not scored" with why, no 🟥, not recorded, tried again at the next read. Old scorer: `POSTHOC_SCORER` 2 in the session file (`prScorer`) and in each new alert; a listed PR this session knows that has a `pr-<n>.json` alert without `scorer` (not withdrawn) is scored once more, that alert rewritten `withdrawn: true` with why, and its open PENDING.md line ticked `- [x] … withdrawn <time>: …`. A53: `Issue.group` (rule 1 "named by intent <slug>" / "edited by <lane> too", rule 3 "outside the paths the intent names"); `accept.cappedItems`: ≤ 3 issues one per line, else folder groups as deep as keeps them within three (else the depth whose first three cover most), `<folder> · <n> files: <group>`, then `and N more (/a5r accept lists them all)`; `acceptText` (the refusal) uses it per rule; the card row is a column: the mark and the rule's name on their own line (the name in a growing, wrapping box), then the items indented 2 and wrapping; `/a5r accept` keeps the full list. - S88 (rev 30 re-review, 2026-10-16): the orchestrator's re-review (read-only, probes) found regressions in S86/S87; fixed as decided, still 0.18.0. (1) `parseLockLine`: a free-form holder comes only from `session <8 hex>` or the token right after `held by` (a 6-8 hex id with a digit, else `heldLane`, the first word up to a space, `(`, `since`, `until`); the scan for an 8-hex word anywhere is gone; `namesHolder` matches a lane only through `heldLane`. (2) `isUnresolvedHand`: a readable hand line whose holder is no single known session: `editorProblem` refuses on the name alone with `HAND_CONVERT_ALT`; `convertHandLock` (editor request) turns it into a standard HELD line for the requester when no alive peer holds a lease and the line's PID is not running (the queue and the launch gate as for a free lock); `decide`'s wait names the same step. (3) `handLapseOf`: `HAND_LAPSE_MS` 30 min past the end for an unresolved holder (10 min and holder not alive for a resolved one), note `hand-written lock by <lane> lapsed: past <end> + 30 min, nothing running`. (4) `A5R.writesProtected`: the 0.17.1 model with `isKitCheck` (the allow-list) and READERS; a redirect or tee into the kit refused even for them. (5) the unchecked grant is removed: `decide` waits `probe` naming `FREE_RAM_READ` and `freeGb`; the editor tool takes `freeGb` on any action (`supplied`, 2 min), `editorStep` decides with it after a failed fresh retry and `logSupplied` writes `rule=ram-supplied` once per number and use; the PIE check retries once, then uses a supplied number or refuses with the step; `ramStep` sends `NOTICES.pieProbeFailed` while PIE runs and the last probe failed (`probeOk`). (6) `missingLockStep` re-reads the lock after its FREE write; `isUnrealProc`/`isEditorProc` count every `UnrealEditor*` (the probe's PowerShell also lists `UnrealEditor*.exe`). (7) `mcpCallKind`: any console method → write (PIE when its command starts PIE); a method naming quit, exit, close, shutdown or save → write. (8) B5 and B10 noted under the A88 verdicts. README (0.18.0 entry, the hand-written lock, the kit allow-list, RAM) and rules text. - S105 (rev 35, 2026-10-20): adversarial self-check of the change (no code change from it). (1) A fetch that hangs or prompts: env `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`, no stdin, killed at 5 min by `$.process.run` (read as a failed fetch: told, the list STALE); it runs off the tick chain, so no hook and no Editor request waits on it (engine test with the fetch held). Residual: a `core.askPass` GUI helper set on the machine could still open a window until the kill (none set by default); a fetch killed on Windows may leave a `git-remote-https` child, which the RAM step's orphan-git reaper handles (README Giới hạn). No LFS object: a plain fetch never smudges and repo hooks are off; `GIT_LFS_SKIP_SMUDGE` is not set. (2) A fetch during a running merge or with `.git/index.lock`: fetch writes only `refs/remotes/origin/main` (and its reflog) and `FETCH_HEAD`, never the index or the working tree, so neither blocks it nor is touched by it; the worker still refuses MERGE_HEAD and an old index.lock (rule 4). A concurrent fetch by another lane's process can fail a ref lock: that reads as a failed fetch (told, STALE), never a wait. (3) A fetch racing the worker's own fetch at T: the worker starts only when no A5R dry-run runs (`dryStep`'s `isBusy`; the job awaits its fetch), and no round runs once `workerAt` is set; a reload of the mod mid-fetch forgets `dryJob` and may start T's round again beside an orphaned fetch: at worst a ref-lock failure, read as a failed fetch, and the worker still waits for the new round. (4) The T dry-run delaying the worker past the hard end: T's round is fetch ≤ 5 min + rev-parse + merge-tree ≤ 1 min + rule 11 ≤ 3 min (then foreign) + the added-files diff ≤ 1 min, about 10.5 min at worst; it starts only within 10 min of T; a cutoff round still running at T is waited for (≤ 10.5 min) and a list it writes after T is T's; the worst case (a cutoff round that fails just before T + 10, then T's round) starts the worker about T + 21 min, leaving ≥ 24 min of a 45-min freeze (the worker's task names the hard end). (5) Owners told twice: each session names a path once per sync (`pushNamed`), tests above; a reload between the cutoff and T may name a path once more (documented in the code). Also checked: a sync moved while a dry-run runs drops that result (`onSyncFile` checks id, time, state and holder); a dry-run of a moved sync waits for the old one to end (one at a time).

- S106 (rev 35, 2026-10-20): the Fable adversary review of 2ed91f2/f5bb4ac ("fix first", 483/483 pass). (1, high, A108) the hand-back counted every git.exe on the machine, and E:/Projects/s2 has core.fsmonitor=true, so `git fsmonitor--daemon` always runs (MEASURED here with A5R's own probe: 3 daemons plus 2 other git) and every hand-back would have said "wait" for ever → the RAM probe (already Win32_Process) returns each git process's command line (`Proc.cmd`, git only, ≤ 500 characters); `checkoutGitPids` keeps git whose `-C <path>` (a5r.ts `gitTarget`) is under the checkout (`under`), never the fsmonitor daemon; `.git/index.lock` is the only wait, the PIDs are information; dryrun.ts's comment no longer says that no git outlives A5R's call. (2, medium) a stale index.lock would have meant waiting for ever → `GitBusy = { gitPids, lockAgeMs }` (fs.stat mtime), `gitBusyNote`: 10 min old with no git of the checkout running, or none known, → "remove that stale lock first (rm <checkout>/.git/index.lock)"; else wait (a lock 10 min old then is stale). (3, medium-low) holder-side writes could drop the dry-run's list (writeSync refuses only another holder's write; the conflicts, rules and build actions and messageNoMod wrote a copy read before an await) → one `updateSync` (read-modify-write, only while the same planned sync has the same holder as the copy acted on) serves the dry-run's two writes and those four writers. (4, low) a fetch that fails at once (another fetch's ref lock) is tried once more after 5 s (`$.clock.sleep`); not after a kill at its limit. (5, low) the round at T: chosen the shorter fetch limit at T (`FETCH_MS` 2 min at T, 5 at the cutoff), not moving the hard end: the worker fetches again itself (rule 4), so A5R's fetch at T only freshens the list, and moving the hard end would lengthen every session's freeze and rewrite the lock line; T's round is now at most about 2 + 0.5 + 1 + 3 + 1 ≈ 7.5 min (a quick failure retried adds 5 s and a second ≤ 2 min try only when the first failed fast). (6, low) a reload mid-round: documented (dryrun.ts header, README Giới hạn), not a persisted timestamp: the second fetch then fails on the ref lock, is tried once more (4), and at worst reads as a failed fetch (told, the list stale); a timestamp would add a sync.json write at every round start and a busy rule for a rare case. (7) note only (README already says it). (8, cosmetic) the owners' freeze words: "the worker reads no messages and aborts on a conflict that is not mechanical and no rule settles, then <holder> finishes the merge by hand: tell <holder> how the two sides combine (never discard either side)"; the untracked one likewise. - S107 (rev 36, 2026-10-20): A109, 0.21.2, committed c9f929e (with S108's fixes). - S109 (rev 36, 2026-10-20): adversarial self-check (no code change from it). (1) A path with spaces or `&` in the checkout or engine root: the smoke runs both (`chk o&k`, `eng a&b`) through the task's exact command; inside `set "NAME=…"` and every quoted use `&` is plain text; a `'` in the script path is doubled in the PowerShell literal (unit); `%` and `^` are refused in the log for the checkout and the option (call would change them), and a root argument holding them makes call miss Build.bat, so the log ends with the exit line and no Result: (build-unknown at once); a non-ASCII path may break (cmd reads the .cmd in the console code page; README). (2) A stale script from an earlier sync: the name carries the sync id and the file is written fresh at each worker start (a moved sync keeps its id and is rewritten at its own T); the log is read only when written since `workerAt`, so an earlier run's log or Result: never counts (rev32b's stale-Result test still passes). (3) The script for a non-build sync: none is written and the task says `Build: no` (engine test); the build flag cannot change once frozen (the sync tool refuses `build` in the freeze), so the flag at the spawn is the flag at the worker's end. (4) A reload between writing it and the worker's start: before the sync.json claim the next tick rewrites the same script and claims; after the claim and before the spawn no worker starts (D7's "never starts another", as before this change) but the script on disk and `buildRun` on file stay, so the holder's notices still give the command; after the spawn the worker has its task. (5) `engineRoot` pointing at a wrong engine: both D:/GameEditors/5.8-ninetails-ed5c054 and D:/GameEditors/UE_5.8 hold a Build.bat on this machine (read here), so the script would build with UE_5.8; A5R cannot tell the S2 engine (the registry GUID differs from S2.uproject's EngineAssociation, machine profile) and does not guess: plugin.json and README name the profile's engine and "never D:\GameEditors\UE_5.8", and the log's last line names the engine used. Open: no check of the engine identity; the worker's 60-s check is its own judgement (A5R's log reading backs it).

- S112 (rev 37, 2026-10-21): thermo-nuclear review (AGENTS.md; the thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review-subagent on `git diff intent/a5r -- a5r`, new files included). Pass 1: changes requested, 1 critical finding, fixed before 0f50ebd: (C1) the checkout was spread over five module variables (`givenLock`, `uproject`, `storeDir`, `checkoutFound`, `isS2`), a mutated copy of the options (`opts.editorLock` overwritten with the found lock, the user's value kept twice, a second cast), a lock → root round trip (`s2Root({ ...opts, editorLock })`) and readers correct only after the find → one `Checkout | null` from the pure `checkoutOf` (machine.ts), the options never copied or mutated, `s2Root()`/`lockFile()` and the store paths read the checkout (no opts), `givenLock`/`uproject`/`hasCheckout` and the fake options object gone, the find awaited at the top of every hook that reads the checkout; a wrapper around `on` was tried and rejected: the engine refuses a second registration of one event without a matcher (MEASURED in a scratch mod: `on("tool.call") is registered twice without a matcher`), and wrapping the overloaded `on` needs casts that hide the handler types. Non-critical fixed: (N2) the build script takes the checkout's .uproject and target `<project>Editor`, so a detected project builds consistently; (N3) `EngineFind` a union with `root: null`, `syncWorkerTask`'s engine required, `buildStep('')` no longer claims "no engine root" when the write failed; (N4) the sources read in order, stopping at the first Build.bat; (N6) `/a5r on` re-finds only while none was found, after command.run's own await, so never two finds at once. Pass 2 (a new reviewer with pass 1's findings): approve, no critical finding; its notes applied: one `holdsProject` for the three ".uproject in this folder" checks (one of them read `<root>/` as the project when none was known, kept from harm only by `isS2`), the `Checkout.uproject` comment, the scan's header (the `e.g.` labels; posix paths not scanned). Left open (pass 2's notes): `spawnWorker` re-checks `checkout` for the type's sake; `findEngine`'s break and `pickEngine`'s index encode the order twice (pickEngine stays testable); `POST_MERGE_TESTS` names S2.uproject (S2's documented test command) while the build follows the found project; the scan reads drive-letter paths only.

- S113 (rev 37, 2026-10-21): adversarial self-check (no code change from it beyond S111/S112). (1) A session in an S2 worktree: git's common dir is the main tree's `.git`, so the checkout is the main tree (MEASURED: `E:/Projects/s2-wt-33720` → `E:/Projects/s2`; engine test with `E:/s2-wt/feat`); the worktree's own Saved is never written; the session coordinates (its root holds S2.uproject, as before). (2) A cwd on another drive: the find runs `git -C <cwd>`, so the drive does not matter; ather-mods on D: finds `D:/Projects/ather-mods` with no .uproject → idle with the hint unless `editorLock` is set (Hai's settings will set it: the orchestrator). (3) A repository with its .uproject in a subfolder: not detected, idle with the hint (engine test; README Giới hạn says to set `editorLock`). (4) Git missing or slow: the run rejects or passes 5 s → no checkout, idle; `/a5r on` looks again (engine test); a session start waits at most 5 s once (MEASURED 22-31 ms on S2 here); a cwd change mid-session is not followed (README). (5) S2.log rotated or from another engine: the 3 newest of `<project>.log` and its backups are read; with none left the registry decides (here it would find none: the GUID mismatch), and the task says no engine root was found, so the holder builds by hand or sets `engineRoot`; the log names the engine the Editor last RAN, not necessarily the one that should build: an Editor opened once from another engine makes the next sync build with that engine; the worker's task names the root and its source and the log's exit line names the engine used, so it shows; `engineRoot` pins it (README Giới hạn). (6) Paths with spaces: git runs by argv (no shell); `mainTreeOf` keeps spaces (unit); the script's quoting is unchanged and the smoke runs `chk o&k` and `eng a&b`. (7) A .uproject named differently: the target is `<project>Editor` (Unreal's convention); S2's post-merge test command stays S2's. (8) Reg on a machine without Epic's keys or without reg.exe: the query fails or prints nothing → none. (9) The TEMP store: a non-checkout session's after-the-fact alerts are shared only among sessions on that machine without a checkout (separate from any checkout's Saved/A5R). Open: none of these needs a code change; the field proofs (Hai's next build sync with `engineRoot` unset) are the orchestrator's.

- S114 (rev 37, 2026-10-21): the Fable adversary review of 0f50ebd/24635f6 ("fix first", 517/517 pass), committed 45a7762, after merging intent/a5r (83d82d7: main merged in, ather-automata 0.6.5, no a5r/ change) as c07c4c8 so the release fast-forwards. Gates before (c07c4c8): `✔ Validation passed`, tsc exit 0, `517 pass 0 fail`, scan exit 0 (0 drive-letter paths), smoke exit 0. (D1, medium-high) a failed detection (git off PATH, git < 2.31 without `--path-format`, over 5 s) left no checkout, so rule 1's shared-checkout guards and the Editor-lock gate went quiet with a wrong hint and no retry, and a rejected cached promise would have made every hook throw → register.ts `mainTreeHere` walks up from the session's folder to the first `.git` (a folder with HEAD → that tree; a file → machine.ts `mainTreeOfGitFile`; git only for a `.git` file naming anything else, 5 s), answering a failure as a miss; `findCheckout` returns `{ checkout, miss, storeDir }` and never throws; one `findAgain` takes it in one assignment: once per load through the cached promise (`ensureCheckout`), and while none is found at each minute (`minute`: the timer now starts for every session; the tick only while the session coordinates) and at /a5r on; machine.ts `Miss` (`{ bare } | 'git-silent' | 'no-repo' | { failed }`) and `noCheckoutWhy(miss)` give the reason. (D2, medium) `$.fs.read` refuses files over 4 MiB, so a long Editor run's log gave no engine (and here the registry misses too) → `logText`: `$.fs.stat` first, over the cap `findstr` reads the line, else the source names the log `over 4 MiB, not read`. A deviation from the review's wording, chosen in the thermo-nuclear review: a failed find is retried at the next minute (like a miss) rather than by the next hook, so one function and one cache own the search. - S117 (rev 38, 2026-10-21): adversarial self-check (no code change from it). (1) Starvation of the head by a stream of small requests: each pass slot is ≤ 30 min (the grant capped, extensions refused while the head is queued, the head never asks it to yield), and once the head has waited 2 h (from its requestedAt, a kept A104 place included) nobody passes; when memory then fits, the head is first. Worst case the head is passed for 2 h plus one 30-min slot; a passer that overruns its end keeps the lease as any live holder does (the overrun notice; S2 §2 lapses only a gone holder). (2) Two sessions both believing they may pass: the passer is the first that fits in `queueOf` order, from the session files, the lock, the sync file and the shared probe (`probe.json`), the same in every session (unit: four sessions, one grant, identical queue views); a split can come only from two readings of free RAM taken at different moments (each session re-probes before it takes the lock and publishes the reading); the lock's read-compare-write and re-read lets one take it, and the other reads the HELD line and waits. (3) The pass slot overrunning 30 min: the end written is ≤ 30 min, `extend` refuses past it (engine test); past its end the holder hears the overrun notice like any lease. (4) A launch:false request that does launch: a shell launch (UnrealEditor, Start-Process UnrealEditor, UnrealEditor-Cmd on a project, launch_s2.ps1) is refused in a launch:false pass slot with the alternative; an Editor started another way (MCP, a driver not matched) shows in the next minute's probe and the holder is told to close it (engine test); A5R kills nothing. A launch:false request that is not a pass (the sync's own slots, a head) is unchanged. (5) Wake spam to a session that stays idle: a grant is one notice id (told once); the memory note at most once an hour (its last time read from the delivered ids, which survive a reload), and only while the code is `ram` (engine test: not again 50 min after a wake that started no turn); `clearStaleBusy` waits out A59's 30 s after A5R's own wake. (6) The overnight replay of L-42 under 0.23.0: free RAM 27-29.5 GB, head daa8b2d4 (no PIE, gate 28), then df9d2d62 (PIE, gate 38). While free < 28 the head waits `ram` and the only later request needs 38: it does not fit, so nobody passes; from 2 h of waiting (13:08 on 20/10) nobody would pass anyway; whenever free reached 28 the head itself would have been granted, as 0.22.0 did at 09:27. So no grant would have happened: memory was the real limit. INFERRED (no reading was logged that night): free RAM stayed under 28 GB from 20:06 to 09:27, since daa8b2d4's A5R was alive and granted itself at 09:27 the first time the gate was met. What 0.23.0 changes for such a night: the wait lines name the code, free RAM and the heavy processes (claude ×N) every 15 min, and each idle queued session is told hourly what holds the memory; the decision to free memory (close idle claude sessions) stays a person's.

## A88 verdicts (rev 30)

- (a) Acceptance diff of a long S2 branch: **real, fixed**. MEASURED 2026-10-16 read-only on `E:/Projects/s2` (`HaiHuynh/20261016`, 1,079 ahead / 85 behind origin/main): `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` 297 files in 0.60 s; `git diff -U0 --no-color … -- . <binary excludes>` 3,526,881 bytes (3.36 MiB, 84% of the 4 MiB cap) in 0.40 s, most of it generated text (`docs/intent/enemy-validator/evidence/baseline/baseline.json` 790 KB of added lines, an exec-plan handoff 338 KB); `--numstat` 0.35 s. The 30 s timeout is not the risk (under 1 s); the 4 MiB cap is: a few more evidence files and every PR attempt from such a branch is refused, and the card re-scores it every 5 min (1 s each, not a stall). Fix: a cut whole diff is re-read in path batches; only a file whose own diff passes 4 MiB stays unread and is named. No cache by HEAD sha: a re-score costs about 1 s per 5 min (MEASURED above), not worth a stale-cache risk.
- (b) An Editor request behind the tick's slow work: **real, fixed**. MEASURED: one rule-11 classification on S2 (`rev-parse origin/main:<p>` + `log --raw --max-count=200 HEAD -- <p>`) 1.3 s for a .cpp, 4.1 s for a .uasset; with merge-tree (≤ 60 s) and up to 40 paths that is about 1–3 min, during which the holder's own `editor request` waited (it ran `runTick` twice on the shared chain); the minute's acceptance re-score (gh and git) sat on the same chain for every session. Fix: the request answers from the Editor's own serialized pass (world read, lapse, grant), never behind the dry-run, the score or the judge; the engine test holds merge-tree open and the request answers (0.17.1's code: `STILL WAITING` after 3 s).
- (c) Launch gate vs PIE check on a failed probe: **real inconsistency, fixed** (revised in S88 by the orchestrator: no blind pass; see below). 0.17.1: `decide` waited `probe` for as long as no reading existed (the shared file absent and every fresh probe failing), while `editorProblem` let PIE start silently. Chosen: retry the probe once, fresh; failing again, **allow with a warning** (the grant notice and a `RAM` notice give the command to read free RAM yourself), for both. Why: a probe failure (PowerShell/CIM) is a machine fault the agent cannot fix, so a wait has no end and blocks everyone queued behind; "free memory then retry" cannot be checked without a reading either; the PIE abort under 3 GB and the S2 watchdogs still guard the crash. This replaces E2's "a missing RAM reading fails closed". S88 (orchestrator, after the re-review): allowing blind was withdrawn; after the retry the launch and PIE are refused with a step the agent can do (read free RAM with the given command and pass `freeGb` to the editor tool), the number is read through the normal gate for 2 min and logged in orchestrate.log, and a probe failing while PIE runs is told to the holder.
- B5 (the card's 5-min re-score of a long branch): left as is: about 1 s per run (MEASURED above), not a stall.
- B10 (an intent close refused on a failing score): by design: it is the checklist end (A56); `/a5r pass <slug>` lets one through.
- Review PLAUSIBLE (mcpKind over the whole event JSON): **real, fixed**: `find_assets` with `set_visibility` in its query was a write (refused while another lane holds the Editor); now classed by tool and method only (tests above).
